# M4 App + 3D View Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build the Kerf app a woodworker uses (dark workspace, five workflow steps, panels, scrubby fields, history, 3D view and the seven escape-hatch tools) so the 6-step bookcase end-to-end test passes in a real browser, the resize to 30" passes, and solve stays under 100 ms.

**Architecture:** Three layers depending one way. `@kerf/core` (plus `io`, `geometry`) does all woodworking maths and gains six small public additions. `packages/app/src/model/` holds intent, history, step, selection and shop with no React or DOM, applies named commands and solves after each. `packages/app/src/ui`, `viewport`, `tools` only render the model and send it commands.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 7 strict, React 19, Vite 8, Zustand (vanilla store), three.js through @react-three/fiber, manifold-3d through `@kerf/geometry`, Vitest 5 (+ jsdom, Testing Library), Playwright, Biome, dependency-cruiser.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-m4-app-3d-view-design.md` (read it first). Also binding: `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/CODE_QUALITY.md`, `docs/testing/UNIT_TEST_STANDARD.md`, `docs/testing/INTEGRATION_TEST_STANDARD.md`, `docs/spec/04-interaction-spec.md`.

## Global Constraints

- Lengths are integers (1in = 203200 units, 1/64" = 3175, 1 mm = 8000). No floats for lengths, ever; display meshes (inches as float32) are the only floats and never feed back.
- `packages/core` imports nothing from other packages, the DOM, React or three.js. The app imports `@kerf/core`, never `@kerf/core/internal` (a dependency-cruiser rule added in Task 8 enforces it).
- Save intent, derive geometry: nothing derived is written to `.kerf`; history and guide lines are never saved.
- UI strings use shop language. Banned in any rendered text: mesh, vertex, face (geometry), component, instance, material, extrude, boolean. (Shop words "show face", "inside face" are allowed; a test scans for the banned list in Task 8.)
- Every button carries a text label; no icon-only controls; nothing floats (no floating windows).
- Every source file starts with the licence header:
  ```
  // Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
  // Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
  // SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only
  ```
  (CSS files use the `/* … */` form already in `packages/app/src/styles.css`.)
- Zero lint warnings (`pnpm lint`), zero type errors (`pnpm typecheck`), no dead code, no commented-out code, no `TODO` without an issue number, no scratch files.
- Tests first and seen failing for the right reason; expected values from the spec or a hand calculation written in a comment; never from running the code. No `should` in test names.
- Never edit a golden fixture to make a test pass. The one approved edit to `packages/app/e2e/bookcase.spec.ts` is Task 1's `exact: true`.
- **End-to-end name collisions** (Playwright matches names and labels by case-insensitive substring and counts hidden elements): no rendered `<label>`, `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` text may contain another binding label (`Width`, `Height`, `Depth`, `Count`, `Spacing`, `Radius`, `Carcase stock`, `Top and bottom`, `Back stock`) unless it is that label; no button other than the step switcher may contain `Shape`, `Divide`, `Joinery`, `Build plan`, `New`, or `Bookcase`; project-tree rows are `role="treeitem"`, never buttons; collapsed sections render no children; regions are labelled only `Project`, `Inspector`, `Model`, `Drawer`, `Workflow`, `Tools`, `History`.
- Commit messages end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Commit on branch `m4-app-3d-view` only.

## Review Focus

1. **A size typed that refers to itself or is nonsense** (`width + 1"` in Width, `abc`, `3` with no unit): the field shows the engine's message, keeps the old value, records no history entry, never crashes. Pinned in Task 10 (`setSize`) and Task 15 (`NumberField`).
2. **A damaged or newer `.kerf` opened** (truncated zip, wrong file, schema 2): a shop-language error naming the problem; the current project stays loaded and undo still works. Pinned in Task 22.
3. **Browser storage missing, full, or holding a stale project** (private window, quota error, a saved project the engine now rejects): the app starts clean with one notice, never a blank screen. Pinned in Task 9 (`storage`) and Task 13 (store start-up).
4. **A project blocked by an assembly error** (dado depth 23/32" on 23/32" stock): drawer banner "Not ready to build", tree badge, the boards still drawn as plain blanks with the broken one red; nothing throws when `shapes` is empty. Pinned in Task 2 (placements while blocked), Task 21 (banner), Task 24 (blank drawing).
5. **A drag of a number field over many steps** (200 scrub steps, then release): exactly one history entry, final value exact on the 1/64" grid, solving never lags behind the last value. Pinned in Task 12 (`preview`/`commit`) and Task 15.

**Interfaces:**

- [ ] **Step 1: Write ADR 015**

```markdown
# ADR 015: M4 app and 3D view

Status: Proposed. Every rule below marked "maintainer to confirm" was chosen while designing M4
because the spec is silent; the maintainer approved the design on 2026-10-12 and confirms each
rule at the M4 review.

## Context

The Build plan's M4 builds the app: modules, panels, scrubby fields, history, the viewport and the
escape-hatch tools (Interaction spec). The engine (M1-M3) is complete; the app must call it only
through `@kerf/core`. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-m4-app-3d-view-design.md`.

## Decisions (approved in design, 2026-10-12)

| # | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| D1 | The assembly order is written by the engine (`assemblyOrder`) from the form, so the M5 PDF uses the same list. |
| D2 | Joints are in use from the moment the form exists. "Accept all" sets the form's `joineryReviewed` flag: saved, undoable, kept on resize. It changes no board. |
| D3 | Edge profile on boards a form made changes the form's `shelfRoundover`; on a hand-made or detached board it adds a roundover operation to that board. |
| D4 | The shop is per person and kept in browser storage, never in the project. A first-launch checklist sits in Start beside the templates with the starter shop ticked; it never blocks. |
| D5 | Save/open `.kerf`, the light theme and metric are in M4. |
| D6 | All seven tools, including Board bound to a gap and Tape guide lines. |
| D7 | The look: a segmented step switcher and a hint line on top, docked project tree and history on the left, inspector on the right, tool row under the model, build-plan drawer at the bottom. |
| D8 | `bookcase.spec.ts` uses `getByLabel("Back", { exact: true })`: Playwright matches labels by substring and counts hidden elements, so "Back" also matched "Back stock". |

## Rules (maintainer to confirm)

1. **History** is a list of whole projects, one per named change, kept for the session only and capped at the last 200 changes (the oldest is dropped). Clicking an entry steps back to it; a new change from there drops the later entries. Saving writes the project, not the history.
2. **Dragging a field** previews every step and records one history entry on release. One step is the grid (1/64", 0.1 mm); Shift multiplies by 16; Alt divides by 4 but never below the grid.
3. **A field's text** is kept as the user typed it (an expression), after checking it evaluates to the right kind. A field whose form value names a parameter (`width`) edits that parameter.
4. **Units** are the project's `units` field: switching is a named, undoable change. New projects use the units last chosen.
5. **Assembly order wording.** Case: (1) "Dry-fit the case without glue and check that every joint closes." (2) "Glue {list} into the sides." where {list} names the case's other glued boards in order of first appearance, "N × Name" when there are several, joined "A, B and C". (3) "Square the case: the two diagonals must match. Adjust before the glue sets." (4) when it has a back: "Fit the {Back} last." Box: (1) "Dry-fit the box without glue and check that every corner closes." (2) with a bottom "Glue the corners with the {Bottom} in its groove, not glued, so it can move.", without "Glue the corners." (3) "Square the box: the two diagonals must match. Adjust before the glue sets." With several structures each step starts "Case 2: " (or "Box 2: ") from the second on.
6. **Join proposals.** Two touching boards: the one touched on its broad face is the host. A contact patch reaching the host's end or edge proposes a rabbet; one inside the face proposes a dado. Boards meeting face to face or end to end have no joint, and the reason is said. Boards overlapping at both their ends propose a through dovetail with the first board picked as the pin board. Parameters are left to the spec's defaults. The proposal is tried on a copy of the project; any error that joint causes is returned as the reason instead.
7. **Gap binding** writes the gap as an ordinary expression built from the two boards' position and size expressions (stock thickness where a part takes its thickness from its stock), along the one axis on which they face each other. A member of a row (a pattern) cannot be bound; the user is told to detach it first.
8. **Placements** are reported even when an assembly error withholds the shapes, so the viewport can draw every board as a plain blank and colour the broken one.
9. **Joint summaries** use an empty shop when the shop is invalid (every cut then shows its tool as missing); the `invalid-shop` error is reported beside them.
10. **A shape that cannot be made into a display mesh** is drawn as its blank with a warning badge; the reason is shown in the inspector.
11. **Browser storage** holds the shop, the last units, the theme, section open state and the current project. If storage is unavailable the app says so once and works without it. A stored project the engine rejects is set aside with a notice; the app starts empty.
12. **New tokens** extend `tokens.css` (both themes); nothing in M0's tokens changes.

## Consequences

- New public API: `assemblyOrder`, `proposeJoint`, `gapBetween`; `SolveResult` gains `placements` and `joints`; case and box forms accept `joineryReviewed`.
- Known limits: openings are picked in the project tree, not in the 3D view, because the engine does not report where an opening is; two structures in one project still share the origin (ADR 014); history is not kept across a reload; the PDF and `.skp` import are M5.
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// ADR 015 rule 8: where each placed board sits, for the viewport and selection.

import { type PlacedPart, type Project, solve, type Vec3 } from "@kerf/core";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { loadProject, loadShopProfile } from "../../src/index";

const shop = loadShopProfile();
const IN = 203200;
const T = (23 * IN) / 32; // 3/4 plywood, 23/32" actual = 146050

/** The 8 corners of a shape mapped through its frame (independent of how the engine builds it). */
function mappedCorners(p: PlacedPart): string[] {
  const out: string[] = [];
  for (const u of [0, p.size.length])
    for (const v of [0, p.size.width])
      for (const w of [0, p.size.thickness]) {
        const at = [0, 1, 2].map(
          (k) => (p.frame.origin[k] ?? 0) + u * (p.frame.x[k] ?? 0) + v * (p.frame.y[k] ?? 0) + w * (p.frame.z[k] ?? 0),
        );
        out.push(at.join(","));
      }
  return out.sort();
}

function boxCorners(min: Vec3, max: Vec3): string[] {
  const out: string[] = [];
  for (const x of [min[0], max[0]]) for (const y of [min[1], max[1]]) for (const z of [min[2], max[2]]) out.push([x, y, z].join(","));
  return out.sort();
}

describe("solve().placements", () => {
  const form = loadProject("bookcase", "structure");

  it("places every board of the bookcase once: 2 sides, top, bottom, 4 shelves, back", () => {
    // Operation specs, "Golden test: bookcase": Side ×2, Top/bottom ×2, Shelf ×4, Back ×1 = 9.
    const names = solve(form, shop).placements.map((p) => p.name).sort();
    expect(names).toEqual(["Back", "Bottom", "Left side", "Right side", "Shelf", "Shelf", "Shelf", "Shelf", "Top"]);
  });

  it("puts the right side at the origin, 23/32 thick, 12 deep and 72 high", () => {
    // Interaction spec: case 36" W × 72" H × 12" D; the right side stands at x = 0.
    const right = solve(form, shop).placements.find((p) => p.name === "Right side");
    expect(right?.box).toEqual({ min: [0, 0, 0], max: [T, 12 * IN, 72 * IN] });
  });

  it("puts the left side's inside face at 36 - 23/32", () => {
    // 36" - 23/32" = 7315200 - 146050 = 7169150.
    const left = solve(form, shop).placements.find((p) => p.name === "Left side");
    expect(left?.box.min[0]).toBe(7_169_150);
  });

  it("maps each shape's corners exactly onto its box", () => {
    for (const p of solve(form, shop).placements) expect(mappedCorners(p)).toEqual(boxCorners(p.box.min, p.box.max));
  });

  it("names the form and role that made a board", () => {
    const shelf = solve(form, shop).placements.find((p) => p.name === "Shelf");
    expect(shelf?.source?.role).toBe("shelf");
    expect(shelf?.detached).toBe(false);
    expect(shelf?.pattern?.index).toBeTypeOf("number");
  });

  it("still places every board when an assembly error withholds the shapes", () => {
    // A dado as deep as the side is thick is an error (Operation specs, "Dado": error if ≥ host thickness).
    const broken: Project = structuredClone(form);
    const structure = broken.structures[0];
    if (!structure) throw new Error("form has no structure");
    structure.options.dadoDepth = "23/32in";
    const result = solve(broken, shop);
    expect(result.blocked).toBe(true);
    expect(result.shapes).toEqual([]);
    expect(result.placements).toHaveLength(9);
  });
});
```

After `PartShape`:

```ts
/**
 * Where one placed copy of a board sits (ADR 015, rule 8). Reported whenever the boards can be
 * placed, even when an assembly error withholds the shapes, so a viewer can still draw them.
 */
export interface PlacedPart {
  /** The placed copy: an instance id, or `<patternId>:<index>` for a member of a row. */
  instance: Id;
  /** The shape it is drawn with: a `PartShape.definition`. */
  shape: Id;
  /** The part definition it is a copy of. */
  part: Id;
  name: string;
  /** The finished blank, before any cut. */
  size: { length: Len; width: Len; thickness: Len };
  /** Its bounding box in the project frame. */
  box: { min: Vec3; max: Vec3 };
  /**
   * Where the shape's local origin lands and where its length (x), width (y) and thickness (z)
   * point in the project frame, each a signed unit axis: the point (u, v, w) of the shape sits at
   * origin + u·x + v·y + w·z. A mirrored copy has its x reversed and its origin at the other end.
   */
  frame: { origin: Vec3; x: Vec3; y: Vec3; z: Vec3 };
  /** The structure and role that made it; absent for a board made by hand. */
  source?: { structure: Id; role: string };
  /** True when a board a form made was taken over by hand. */
  detached: boolean;
  /** For a member of a row: the row and its index. */
  pattern?: { id: Id; index: number };
}
```

In `SolveResult`, after `shapes`:

```ts
  /** Every placed copy (ADR 015, rule 8). Empty only when the project itself is invalid. */
  placements: PlacedPart[];
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Fill it in `pipeline.ts`**

Add imports `import { boxOf, toParent } from "./solve/assembly";` and `PlacedPart`, `Vec3` to the outputs import. Add:

```ts
const minus = (a: Vec3, b: Vec3): Vec3 => [a[0] - b[0], a[1] - b[1], a[2] - b[2]];

/** Where every placed copy sits; from the solved model, so it exists even when cuts are withheld. */
function placementsOf(p: Pipeline): PlacedPart[] {
  const defs = new Map((p.project?.parts ?? []).map((d) => [d.id, d]));
  return p.parts.map(({ instance, part }) => {
    const frame = { instance, part };
    const origin = toParent(frame, [0, 0, 0]);
    const def = defs.get(part.id);
    const { min, max } = boxOf(instance);
    const placed: PlacedPart = {
      instance: instance.id,
      shape: p.derived.definitionOf.get(instance.id) ?? part.id,
      part: part.id,
      name: instance.name,
      size: { length: part.length, width: part.width, thickness: part.thickness },
      box: { min, max },
      frame: {
        origin,
        x: minus(toParent(frame, [1, 0, 0]), origin),
        y: minus(toParent(frame, [0, 1, 0]), origin),
        z: minus(toParent(frame, [0, 0, 1]), origin),
      },
      detached: def?.detached === true,
    };
    if (def?.source) placed.source = { structure: def.source.structure, role: def.source.role };
    if (instance.pattern) placed.pattern = { id: instance.pattern.id, index: instance.pattern.index };
    return placed;
  });
}
```

In `solveProject`'s return add `placements: placementsOf(p),` after `shapes`.

- Produces:

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// ADR 015 rule 9: every joint in shop words, with the tool each cut needs.

import { type JointSummary, type ShopProfile, solve } from "@kerf/core";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { loadProject, loadShopProfile } from "../../src/index";

const shop = loadShopProfile();
const IN = 203200;

/** Total cuts by kind, width and depth across every joint. */
function cutsBySetting(joints: JointSummary[]): Record<string, number> {
  const out: Record<string, number> = {};
  for (const j of joints) for (const c of j.cuts) out[`${c.kind} ${c.width}x${c.depth}`] = (out[`${c.kind} ${c.width}x${c.depth}`] ?? 0) + c.count;
  return out;
}

describe("solve().joints", () => {
  const form = loadProject("bookcase", "structure");

  it("lists 8 shelf dados, 4 end rabbets and 2 back rabbets for the bookcase", () => {
    // Operation specs, bookcase: each side has 4 dados 23/32 × 1/4 (×2 sides = 8), 2 end rabbets
    // 23/32 × 1/4 (×2 = 4), and a back rabbet 1/4 wide × 3/8 deep (×2 = 2).
    expect(cutsBySetting(solve(form, shop).joints)).toEqual({
      [`dado ${(23 * IN) / 32}x${IN / 4}`]: 8,
      [`rabbet ${(23 * IN) / 32}x${IN / 4}`]: 4,
      [`rabbet ${IN / 4}x${(3 * IN) / 8}`]: 2,
    });
  });

  it("cuts every bookcase joint on the shop's dado stack", () => {
    // Shop profile: dado stack up to 3/4" (152400), wider than 23/32" and 1/4".
    const tools = solve(form, shop).joints.flatMap((j) => j.cuts.map((c) => [c.tool, c.missing]));
    expect(new Set(tools.map((t) => t.join()))).toEqual(new Set(["dadoStack,false"]));
  });

  it("marks every cut missing in a shop with no dado stack and no straight bit", () => {
    const bare: ShopProfile = { kerf: 25_400, tools: [{ type: "handTools", dovetails: true }] };
    const missing = solve(form, bare).joints.flatMap((j) => j.cuts.map((c) => c.missing));
    expect(missing.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(missing.every(Boolean)).toBe(true);
  });

  it("names the boards each joint joins and the form that made it", () => {
    const dado = solve(form, shop).joints.find((j) => j.type === "dado");
    expect(dado?.guest).toBe("Shelf");
    expect(dado?.source?.role).toBeTypeOf("string");
  });

  it("cuts the box's dovetails with hand tools", () => {
    const box = loadProject("box", "structure");
    const tails = solve(box, shop).joints.filter((j) => j.type === "throughDovetail");
    // Keepsake box: four through-dovetail corners.
    expect(tails.reduce((n, j) => n + j.pairs, 0)).toBe(4);
    expect(tails.every((j) => j.cuts.every((c) => c.tool === "handTools" && !c.missing))).toBe(true);
  });
});
```

```ts
/** One kind of cut a joint needs, how many, and the tool that makes it (or the one the shop lacks). */
export interface JointCut {
  kind: "dado" | "groove" | "rabbet" | "dovetail";
  width: Len;
  depth: Len;
  tool: MachiningSetup["tool"];
  missing: boolean;
  count: number;
}

/** A joint in shop words (ADR 015, rule 9): the two boards, how many pairs, and its cuts. */
export interface JointSummary {
  joint: Id;
  type: Joint["type"];
  /** Shop names of the host and guest boards ("Right side", "Shelf"). */
  host: string;
  guest: string;
  /** Host/guest pairs it makes: a row of 4 shelves in one side is 4. */
  pairs: number;
  cuts: JointCut[];
  /** The structure and role that made it; absent for a joint made by hand. */
  source?: GeneratedFrom;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Export `toolFor` and summarise in `pipeline.ts`**

In `machining.ts` change `function toolFor(` to `export function toolFor(` and give it a doc comment: `/** The tool that makes a cut in this shop, or the one it would need (\`missing\`). */`.

In `pipeline.ts`:

```ts
const NO_TOOLS: ShopProfile = { kerf: 0, tools: [] };

/** Each joint with its cuts grouped by kind and size (ADR 015, rule 9). */
function jointSummariesOf(p: Pipeline, shop: ShopProfile | undefined): JointSummary[] {
  if (!p.project) return [];
  const names = new Map<Id, string>();
  for (const i of p.project.instances) names.set(i.id, i.name ?? i.id);
  for (const row of p.project.patterns) names.set(row.id, names.get(row.source) ?? row.id);
  return p.project.joints.map((joint) => {
    const pairs = p.derived.pairs.filter((pair) => pair.joint === joint.id);
    const cuts = new Map<string, JointCut>();
    for (const cut of pairs.flatMap((pair) => pair.machined)) {
      const key = `${cut.kind}|${cut.width}|${cut.depth}`;
      const found = cuts.get(key);
      if (found) found.count += 1;
      else cuts.set(key, { kind: cut.kind, width: cut.width, depth: cut.depth, ...toolFor(cut, shop ?? NO_TOOLS), count: 1 });
    }
    const summary: JointSummary = {
      joint: joint.id,
      type: joint.type,
      host: names.get(joint.host) ?? joint.host,
      guest: names.get(joint.guest) ?? joint.guest,
      pairs: pairs.length,
      cuts: [...cuts.values()],
    };
    if (joint.generatedBy) summary.source = joint.generatedBy;
    return summary;
  });
}
```

Add `joints: jointSummariesOf(p, copy),` to `solveProject`'s return.

`packages/core/src/outputs/assembly-order.ts`:

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// Assembly order (Interaction spec, "The build plan", item 6: "comes from the structure in the
// prototype (case: dry-fit, glue carcase, square it, fit back last)"). Wording: ADR 015, rule 5.

import type { AssemblyStep } from "../outputs";
import { runPipeline } from "../pipeline";
import type { SolvedInstance } from "../solve/model";
import type { Id, Project, ShopProfile, Structure } from "../types";

interface Board {
  id: Id;
  name: string;
  role: string;
}

/** "A", "A and B", "A, B and C". */
function listed(names: string[]): string {
  if (names.length <= 1) return names.join("");
  return `${names.slice(0, -1).join(", ")} and ${names.at(-1)}`;
}

/** Names in first-appearance order, "N × Name" when a name repeats. */
function counted(boards: Board[]): string {
  const counts = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const b of boards) counts.set(b.name, (counts.get(b.name) ?? 0) + 1);
  return listed([...counts].map(([name, n]) => (n > 1 ? `${n} × ${name}` : name)));
}

function caseSteps(boards: Board[]): AssemblyStep[] {
  const all = boards.map((b) => b.id);
  const sides = boards.filter((b) => b.role === "side");
  const glued = boards.filter((b) => b.role !== "side" && b.role !== "back");
  const back = boards.find((b) => b.role === "back");
  const steps: AssemblyStep[] = [
    { text: "Dry-fit the case without glue and check that every joint closes.", parts: all },
    { text: `Glue ${counted(glued)} into the sides.`, parts: [...glued, ...sides].map((b) => b.id) },
    { text: "Square the case: the two diagonals must match. Adjust before the glue sets.", parts: all },
  ];
  if (back) steps.push({ text: `Fit the ${back.name} last.`, parts: [back.id] });
  return steps;
}

function boxSteps(boards: Board[]): AssemblyStep[] {
  const all = boards.map((b) => b.id);
  const bottom = boards.find((b) => b.role === "bottom");
  return [
    { text: "Dry-fit the box without glue and check that every corner closes.", parts: all },
    bottom
      ? { text: `Glue the corners with the ${bottom.name} in its groove, not glued, so it can move.`, parts: all }
      : { text: "Glue the corners.", parts: all },
    { text: "Square the box: the two diagonals must match. Adjust before the glue sets.", parts: all },
  ];
}

/**
 * The order to put each structure together, from its form. Empty for a project with no structure
 * or one that cannot be solved. Never throws.
 */
export function assemblyOrder(project: Project, shop: ShopProfile): AssemblyStep[] {
  const p = runPipeline(project, shop);
  if (!p.project) return [];
  const sources = new Map(p.project.parts.map((d) => [d.id, d.source]));
  const boardsOf = (structure: Structure): Board[] =>
    p.model.instances.flatMap((i: SolvedInstance) => {
      const source = sources.get(i.def);
      return source?.structure === structure.id ? [{ id: i.id, name: i.name, role: source.role }] : [];
    });
  return p.project.structures.flatMap((structure, index) => {
    const steps = structure.type === "case" ? caseSteps(boardsOf(structure)) : boxSteps(boardsOf(structure));
    if (index === 0) return steps;
    const prefix = `${structure.type === "case" ? "Case" : "Box"} ${index + 1}: `;
    return steps.map((s) => ({ ...s, text: prefix + s.text }));
  });
}
```

`api.ts`: re-export with its contract:

```ts
export { assemblyOrder } from "./outputs/assembly-order";
```

`index.ts` already re-exports `./api` and `./outputs` (so `AssemblyStep` is exported). If `pipeline.ts` imports from `outputs/` and this creates a cycle flagged by `pnpm lint:deps`, move `assemblyOrder` into `api.ts` as a thin wrapper that calls a pure `assemblySteps(project, instances)` in `outputs/assembly-order.ts` instead.

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// Interaction spec, "3D tools": Join proposes the joint from the contact and the shop.
// Rules: ADR 015, rule 6. Defaults: Operation specs (dado depth = host thickness / 3 snapped down;
// rabbet depth = host thickness / 2 snapped down; width = guest thickness).

import { generateStructures, type Id, type Project, proposeJoint, type ShopProfile } from "@kerf/core";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { loadProject, loadShopProfile } from "../../src/index";

const shop = loadShopProfile();
const JOINT = "01K7M3Q8ZD00000000T9000001";
const SHELF = "01K7M3Q8ZD00000000T9000002";

/** The bookcase's boards as hand-made boards with no joints, plus one loose shelf at 36". */
function looseBoards(): Project {
  const form = loadProject("bookcase", "structure");
  const structure = form.structures[0];
  if (!structure) throw new Error("no structure");
  structure.openings = [{ id: structure.openings[0]?.id ?? "", children: [] }];
  const g = generateStructures(form, shop);
  const instances = g.instances.map(({ generatedBy: _, ...i }) => i);
  const top = instances.find((i) => i.name === "Top");
  if (!top) throw new Error("no top");
  instances.push({ ...top, id: SHELF, name: "Loose shelf", position: { ...top.position, z: "36in" } });
  return {
    ...g,
    structures: [],
    joints: [],
    matchSets: [],
    patterns: [],
    parts: g.parts.map(({ source: _s, detached: _d, ...p }) => p),
    instances,
  };
}

const idOf = (p: Project, name: string): Id => {
  const found = p.instances.find((i) => i.name === name);
  if (!found) throw new Error(`no ${name}`);
  return found.id;
};

describe("proposeJoint()", () => {
  it("proposes a dado for a shelf meeting the middle of a side, 15/64 deep", () => {
    // 23/32 ÷ 3 = 23/96" = 15.33/64 → snapped down to 15/64" = 47625 units.
    const p = looseBoards();
    const r = proposeJoint(p, shop, idOf(p, "Right side"), SHELF, JOINT);
    expect(r).toMatchObject({ ok: true, joint: { type: "dado", host: idOf(p, "Right side"), guest: SHELF }, tool: "dadoStack", missing: false });
    if (r.ok) expect(r.description).toBe('Loose shelf in a dado in Right side, 23/32" wide and 15/64" deep');
  });

  it("proposes a rabbet for the top meeting the end of a side, 23/64 deep", () => {
    // 23/32 ÷ 2 = 23/64" exactly.
    const p = looseBoards();
    const r = proposeJoint(p, shop, idOf(p, "Top"), idOf(p, "Right side"), JOINT);
    expect(r).toMatchObject({ ok: true, joint: { type: "rabbet", host: idOf(p, "Right side"), guest: idOf(p, "Top") } });
    if (r.ok) expect(r.description).toBe('Top in a rabbet in Right side, 23/32" wide and 23/64" deep');
  });

  it("says the tool is missing in a shop with no dado stack or straight bit", () => {
    const bare: ShopProfile = { kerf: 25_400, tools: [] };
    const p = looseBoards();
    expect(proposeJoint(p, bare, idOf(p, "Right side"), SHELF, JOINT)).toMatchObject({ ok: true, missing: true });
  });

  it("refuses two boards that do not touch, and says so", () => {
    const p = looseBoards();
    expect(proposeJoint(p, shop, idOf(p, "Top"), SHELF, JOINT)).toEqual({
      ok: false,
      reason: "Top and Loose shelf do not touch, so there is nothing to join.",
    });
  });

  it("refuses a board that is not in the project", () => {
    const p = looseBoards();
    expect(proposeJoint(p, shop, "01K7M3Q8ZD00000000T9000099", SHELF, JOINT)).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
  });

  it("tries a preferred type and refuses it with the engine's reason when it cannot seat", () => {
    // A shelf in the middle of a side cannot sit in a rabbet (ADR 013: a rabbet's guest lies
    // wholly inside the step at the host's end or edge).
    const p = looseBoards();
    const r = proposeJoint(p, shop, idOf(p, "Right side"), SHELF, JOINT, "rabbet");
    expect(r.ok).toBe(false);
  });

  it("does not change the project it is given", () => {
    const p = looseBoards();
    const before = JSON.stringify(p);
    proposeJoint(p, shop, idOf(p, "Right side"), SHELF, JOINT);
    expect(JSON.stringify(p)).toBe(before);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `packages/core/src/joints/propose.ts`**

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// The Join tool's proposal (Interaction spec, "3D tools": "the joint is proposed from the contact
// and the shop profile"). Rules: ADR 015, rule 6. The proposal is tried on a copy of the project
// so a joint the engine would reject is never offered.

import { formatLength } from "../length";
import type { MachiningSetup } from "../outputs";
import { toolFor } from "../outputs/machining";
import { runPipeline } from "../pipeline";
import { boxOf, boxToLocal, contactOf, type Frame, faceAt } from "../solve/assembly";
import type { Id, Joint, Project, ShopProfile } from "../types";

export type JointProposal =
  | { ok: true; joint: Joint; tool: MachiningSetup["tool"]; missing: boolean; description: string }
  | { ok: false; reason: string };

const refuse = (reason: string): JointProposal => ({ ok: false, reason });

/** Host is the board touched on its broad face; null when neither or both are. */
function hostAndGuest(a: Frame, b: Frame, axis: 0 | 1 | 2, at: number): [Frame, Frame] | string {
  const fa = faceAt(a, axis, at);
  const fb = faceAt(b, axis, at);
  const names = `${a.instance.name} and ${b.instance.name}`;
  if (!fa || !fb) return `${names} do not sit flat against each other.`;
  const aBroad = fa.axis === 2;
  const bBroad = fb.axis === 2;
  if (aBroad && bBroad) return `${names} lie face to face; glue them, there is no joint to cut.`;
  if (!aBroad && !bBroad) return `${names} meet end to end; Kerf has no joint for that yet.`;
  return aBroad ? [a, b] : [b, a];
}

/**
 * Proposes a joint between two placed boards (ids from `solve(...).placements`), with the tool the
 * shop would use or the one it lacks. Never throws; `jointId` names the joint if it is accepted.
 */
export function proposeJoint(
  project: Project,
  shop: ShopProfile,
  first: Id,
  second: Id,
  jointId: Id,
  prefer?: Joint["type"],
): JointProposal {
  const p = runPipeline(project, shop);
  if (!p.project) return refuse("The project has a problem that stops Kerf placing its boards; fix it first.");
  const frameOf = (id: Id): Frame | undefined => {
    const cut = p.parts.find((c) => c.instance.id === id);
    return cut && { instance: cut.instance, part: cut.part };
  };
  const a = frameOf(first);
  const b = frameOf(second);
  if (!a || !b) return refuse("Pick two boards to join.");
  for (const f of [a, b]) {
    if (f.instance.pattern) return refuse(`${f.instance.name} is one of a row; detach it first to join it on its own.`);
  }

  const contact = contactOf(boxOf(a.instance), boxOf(b.instance));
  let type: Joint["type"];
  let host: Frame;
  let guest: Frame;
  if (contact.kind === "touch") {
    const pair = hostAndGuest(a, b, contact.axis, contact.patch.min[contact.axis]);
    if (typeof pair === "string") return refuse(pair);
    [host, guest] = pair;
    const patch = boxToLocal(host, contact.patch);
    const atEnd = patch.min[0] === 0 || patch.max[0] === host.part.length;
    const atEdge = patch.min[1] === 0 || patch.max[1] === host.part.width;
    type = atEnd || atEdge ? "rabbet" : "dado";
  } else if (contact.kind === "overlap") {
    [host, guest] = [a, b];
    type = "throughDovetail";
  } else {
    return refuse(`${a.instance.name} and ${b.instance.name} do not touch, so there is nothing to join.`);
  }

  if (prefer) type = prefer;
  const joint: Joint = { id: jointId, type, host: host.instance.id, guest: guest.instance.id, params: {} };
  const trial = runPipeline({ ...p.project, joints: [...p.project.joints, joint] }, shop);
  const problem = trial.diagnostics.find((d) => d.severity === "error" && d.joint === jointId);
  if (problem) return refuse(problem.message);
  const cut = trial.derived.pairs.find((pair) => pair.joint === jointId)?.machined[0];
  if (!cut) return refuse(`Kerf could not work out the cuts for ${guest.instance.name}.`);
  const { tool, missing } = toolFor(cut, shop);
  const what = type === "dado" ? "a dado" : type === "rabbet" ? "a rabbet" : "through dovetails";
  const size = `${formatLength(cut.width, project.units)} wide and ${formatLength(cut.depth, project.units)} deep`;
  return { ok: true, joint, tool, missing, description: `${guest.instance.name} in ${what} in ${host.instance.name}, ${size}` };
}
```

### Task 7: A board that fits a gap (`gapBetween`)

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/core/src/solve/gap.ts`
- Modify: `packages/core/src/api.ts`
- Test: `packages/fixtures/tests/unit/gap-between.unit.test.ts`, `packages/fixtures/tests/prop/gap-binding.prop.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `solveModel`/`runPipeline` instances, `axisMaps` (`solve/assembly.ts`).
- Produces:
  ```ts
  export type Gap =
    | { ok: true; axis: "x" | "y" | "z"; start: Expr; length: Expr }
    | { ok: false; reason: string };
  export function gapBetween(project: Project, a: Id, b: Id): Gap
  ```
  `start` is the expression for the near face of the gap (the end of `a` or `b`, whichever is lower along the axis); `length` the clear distance. Both are plain expressions over parameters and stock, so a board using them follows any resize.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// Interaction spec, "3D tools": "drawn between two parts, its length binds to the gap".
// Rules: ADR 015, rule 7.

import { gapBetween, generateStructures, type Id, type Project, solveModel } from "@kerf/core";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { handMadeBookcase as handMade, loadProject, loadShopProfile, withParam } from "../../src/index";

const shop = loadShopProfile();

const idOf = (p: Project, name: string): Id => {
  const found = p.instances.find((i) => i.name === name);
  if (!found) throw new Error(`no ${name}`);
  return found.id;
};

/** The gap's length worked out by the engine, through a parameter that uses it. */
function lengthOf(p: Project, expr: string): number {
  const model = solveModel({ ...p, params: [...p.params, { id: "01K7M3Q8ZD00000000T9000010", name: "gap", expr, kind: "length" }] });
  const q = model.params.gap;
  if (!q) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(model.diagnostics));
  return Number(q.value.num / q.value.den);
}

describe("gapBetween()", () => {
  it("measures 34-9/16 between the sides of the 36-inch bookcase", () => {
    // Operation specs: 36 − 2 × 23/32 = 34-9/16" = 7315200 − 292100 = 7023100 units.
    const p = handMade();
    const gap = gapBetween(p, idOf(p, "Right side"), idOf(p, "Left side"));
    expect(gap).toMatchObject({ ok: true, axis: "x" });
    if (gap.ok) expect(lengthOf(p, gap.length)).toBe(7_023_100);
  });

  it("follows the width: 28-9/16 at 30 inches", () => {
    // Operation specs, resize checks: 30 − 2 × 23/32 = 28-9/16" = 5803900.
    const p = handMade();
    const gap = gapBetween(p, idOf(p, "Right side"), idOf(p, "Left side"));
    if (!gap.ok) throw new Error(gap.reason);
    expect(lengthOf(withParam(p, "width", "30in"), gap.length)).toBe(5_803_900);
  });

  it("starts the gap at the right side's inside face, 23/32 from the origin", () => {
    const p = handMade();
    const gap = gapBetween(p, idOf(p, "Left side"), idOf(p, "Right side"));
    if (!gap.ok) throw new Error(gap.reason);
    expect(lengthOf(p, gap.start)).toBe(146_050);
  });

  it("refuses two boards that do not face each other across a gap", () => {
    const p = handMade();
    expect(gapBetween(p, idOf(p, "Top"), idOf(p, "Right side"))).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
  });

  it("refuses a member of a row and says to detach it", () => {
    const form = loadProject("bookcase", "structure");
    const g = generateStructures(form, shop);
    const rowId = g.patterns[0]?.id;
    expect(gapBetween(g, `${rowId}:0`, idOf(g, "Right side"))).toEqual({
      ok: false,
      reason: expect.stringContaining("detach"),
    });
  });
});
```

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// Invariant (ADR 015, rule 7): a bound gap always equals the clear distance between the two
// boards' facing faces after any resize. Oracle: width − 2 × 23/32 (hand calculation, Operation
// specs), checked against the engine's own placements.

import { gapBetween, solve, solveModel } from "@kerf/core";
import fc from "fast-check";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { handMadeBookcase, loadShopProfile, RUNS, withParam } from "../../src/index";

const shop = loadShopProfile();
const T = 146_050; // 23/32"

describe("gap binding", () => {
  it("equals the clear distance between the sides for every width from 12 to 96 inches", () => {
    const base = handMadeBookcase();
    const id = (name: string): string => base.instances.find((i) => i.name === name)?.id ?? "";
    const gap = gapBetween(base, id("Right side"), id("Left side"));
    if (!gap.ok) throw new Error(gap.reason);
    fc.assert(
      fc.property(fc.integer({ min: 12 * 64, max: 96 * 64 }), (sixtyFourths) => {
        const p = withParam(base, "width", `${sixtyFourths}/64in`);
        const measured = solveModel({
          ...p,
          params: [...p.params, { id: "01K7M3Q8ZD00000000T9000011", name: "gap", expr: gap.length, kind: "length" }],
        }).params.gap;
        const places = solve(p, shop).placements;
        const right = places.find((x) => x.name === "Right side");
        const left = places.find((x) => x.name === "Left side");
        if (!measured || !right || !left) throw new Error("gap or sides missing");
        const value = Number(measured.value.num / measured.value.den);
        expect(value).toBe(sixtyFourths * 3175 - 2 * T);
        expect(value).toBe(left.box.min[0] - right.box.max[0]);
      }),
      { numRuns: RUNS },
    );
  });
});
```

```ts
/**
 * The reference bookcase form's sides, top, bottom and back as hand-made boards with no joints and
 * no shelves: a plain project for tools that work between boards (gap binding, Join).
 */
export function handMadeBookcase(): Project {
  const g = generateStructures(loadProject("bookcase", "structure"), loadShopProfile());
  return {
    ...g,
    structures: [],
    joints: [],
    matchSets: [],
    patterns: [],
    parts: g.parts.map(({ source: _s, detached: _d, ...p }) => p),
    instances: g.instances
      .filter((i) => !i.name?.startsWith("Shelf"))
      .map(({ generatedBy: _g, ...i }) => i),
  };
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `packages/core/src/solve/gap.ts`**

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// The gap between two boards as an ordinary expression (ADR 015, rule 7), so a board drawn between
// them (Interaction spec, "Board": "its length binds to the gap") follows every resize without
// any change to the expression language. Built from the boards' own position and size expressions.

import type { Expr, Id, Instance, PartDef, Project } from "../types";
import { axisMaps } from "./assembly";
import { solveModel, type SolvedInstance } from "./model";

export type Gap =
  | { ok: true; axis: "x" | "y" | "z"; start: Expr; length: Expr }
  | { ok: false; reason: string };

const AXIS = ["x", "y", "z"] as const;

/** The expression for a part's size along one of its own axes (0 length, 1 width, 2 thickness). */
function sizeExpr(project: Project, part: PartDef, local: 0 | 1 | 2): Expr {
  if (local === 0) return `(${part.length})`;
  if (local === 1) return `(${part.width})`;
  if (part.thickness !== undefined) return `(${part.thickness})`;
  const stock = project.stock.find((s) => s.id === part.stock);
  return `stock["${(stock?.name ?? "").replaceAll('"', '\\"')}"].thickness`;
}

/** The far face of `instance` along a project axis, as an expression. */
function farFace(project: Project, inst: Instance, solved: SolvedInstance, axis: 0 | 1 | 2): Expr {
  const part = project.parts.find((d) => d.id === inst.def) as PartDef;
  const local = axisMaps(solved).findIndex((m) => m.axis === axis) as 0 | 1 | 2;
  return `(${inst.position[AXIS[axis]]}) + ${sizeExpr(project, part, local)}`;
}

const overlaps = (a: SolvedInstance, b: SolvedInstance, k: "x" | "y" | "z"): boolean =>
  a.position[k] < b.position[k] + b.extent[k] && b.position[k] < a.position[k] + a.extent[k];

/** The clear gap between two placed boards, as expressions; never throws. */
export function gapBetween(project: Project, a: Id, b: Id): Gap {
  const model = solveModel(project);
  const sa = model.instances.find((i) => i.id === a);
  const sb = model.instances.find((i) => i.id === b);
  if (!sa || !sb) return { ok: false, reason: "Pick two boards to measure between." };
  for (const s of [sa, sb]) {
    if (s.pattern) return { ok: false, reason: `${s.name} is one of a row; detach it first to measure from it.` };
  }
  const ia = project.instances.find((i) => i.id === a);
  const ib = project.instances.find((i) => i.id === b);
  if (!ia || !ib) return { ok: false, reason: "Pick two boards to measure between." };

  for (const axis of [0, 1, 2] as const) {
    const k = AXIS[axis];
    const others = AXIS.filter((x) => x !== k);
    if (!others.every((o) => overlaps(sa, sb, o))) continue;
    const [low, lowSolved, high] = sa.position[k] + sa.extent[k] <= sb.position[k] ? [ia, sa, ib] : [ib, sb, ia];
    const highSolved = high === ia ? sa : sb;
    if (lowSolved.position[k] + lowSolved.extent[k] > highSolved.position[k]) continue;
    const start = farFace(project, low, lowSolved, axis);
    return { ok: true, axis: k, start, length: `(${high.position[k]}) - (${start})` };
  }
  return { ok: false, reason: `${sa.name} and ${sb.name} do not face each other across a gap.` };
}
```

Export from `api.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * The clear gap between two placed boards as expressions (ADR 015, rule 7): where it starts and how
 * long it is, along the one axis on which they face each other. Never throws.
 */
export { type Gap, gapBetween } from "./solve/gap";
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement `packages/app/e2e/support/banned-words.ts`**

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// The words the interface must never show (CLAUDE.md, "Hard rules"; Interaction spec, "Shop
// language"). Used by the screen tests and by the end-to-end wording check.

export const BANNED_WORDS = [
  "mesh", "vertex", "face", "component", "instance", "material", "extrude", "boolean",
] as const;

/** Shop phrases that use "face" the way a woodworker does (Interaction spec, "Shop language"). */
const SHOP_FACE = /\b(show|inside|outside) faces?\b|\bface to face\b/gi;

/** The banned words found in `text` (whole words, any case, singular or plural), in list order. */
export function bannedWordsIn(text: string): string[] {
  const plain = text.replace(SHOP_FACE, " ");
  return BANNED_WORDS.filter((word) => new RegExp(`\\b${word}(es|s)?\\b`, "i").test(plain));
}
```

```ts
    expect(s.read("kerf.units")).toBe("metric");
  });

  it("is unavailable, and never throws, when the browser has no storage", () => {
    const s = createStorage(undefined);
    expect(s.available).toBe(false);
    expect(s.write("kerf.units", "metric")).toBe(false);
    expect(s.read("kerf.units")).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it("reports a failed write when storage is full", () => {
    expect(createStorage(memory(true)).write("kerf.project", "{}")).toBe(false);
  });

  it("returns undefined for a key never written", () => {
    expect(createStorage(memory()).read("kerf.theme")).toBeUndefined();
  });
});
```

`model/ids.ts`:

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// Ids for what the user makes in the app (a board, a joint, a roundover). The engine requires ULIDs
// (Domain model: "ULID, stable, never reused"); the app, unlike core, may read the clock.

import type { Id } from "@kerf/core";

const CROCKFORD = "0123456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ";

const randomBytes = (n: number): Uint8Array => crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(n));

/** A new ULID: 48 bits of milliseconds then 80 random bits, as 26 Crockford base-32 characters. */
export function newId(now: number = Date.now(), random: (n: number) => Uint8Array = randomBytes): Id {
  let time = now;
  const head: string[] = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i += 1) {
    head.unshift(CROCKFORD[time % 32] as string);
    time = Math.floor(time / 32);
  }
  const tail = [...random(16)].map((byte) => CROCKFORD[byte % 32] as string);
  return head.join("") + tail.join("");
}
```

(The "differs in the same millisecond" test uses real randomness; that is the behaviour under test.)

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// What a new user starts with (design spec, "Shop and stock"): the reference projects' stock and
// the reference shop profile, so the first bookcase matches the golden cut list exactly.

import type { Id, ShopProfile, STOCK_OPTIONS, Stock } from "@kerf/core";

const IN = 203_200;

export const STOCK_IDS = {
  plywood34: "01K7M4AP000000000000000001",
  plywood14: "01K7M4AP000000000000000002",
  walnut44: "01K7M4AP000000000000000003",
} as const satisfies Record<string, Id>;

const SHEET_4X8 = { length: 96 * IN, width: 48 * IN, grain: "along-length" } as const;

/** Copied into each new project, so a saved file always carries its own stock. */
export const STARTER_STOCK: readonly Stock[] = [
  { id: STOCK_IDS.plywood34, name: "3/4 plywood", kind: "sheet", thickness: (23 * IN) / 32, sheet: { ...SHEET_4X8 }, allowance: { length: 0, width: 0 } },
  { id: STOCK_IDS.plywood14, name: "1/4 plywood", kind: "sheet", thickness: IN / 4, sheet: { ...SHEET_4X8 }, allowance: { length: 0, width: 0 } },
  { id: STOCK_IDS.walnut44, name: "4/4 walnut", kind: "solid", thickness: IN / 2, solid: { standardLengths: [96 * IN], maxWidth: 8 * IN }, allowance: { length: 0, width: IN / 4 } },
];

/** Which starter stock each form field uses when a template starts a project. */
export const STOCK_FOR: Partial<Record<(typeof STOCK_OPTIONS)[number], Id>> = {
  carcaseStock: STOCK_IDS.plywood34,
  backStock: STOCK_IDS.plywood14,
  wallStock: STOCK_IDS.walnut44,
  bottomStock: STOCK_IDS.plywood14,
};

/** 1/8" kerf, a dado stack up to 3/4", a router with a 1/8" roundover bit, dovetail hand tools. */
export const STARTER_SHOP: ShopProfile = {
  kerf: IN / 8,
  tools: [
    { type: "dadoStack", maxWidth: (3 * IN) / 4 },
    { type: "router", straightBits: [], roundoverBits: [IN / 8] },
    { type: "handTools", dovetails: true },
  ],
};
```

`model/storage.ts`:

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// Browser storage that never throws (ADR 015, rule 11). A private window, a full store or blocked
// site data must not stop the app; callers learn from `available` and from `write`'s result.

export type StorageKey =
  | "kerf.shop"
  | "kerf.units"
  | "kerf.theme"
  | "kerf.sections"
  | "kerf.project"
  | "kerf.project.setAside";

export interface AppStorage {
  /** False when the browser gives no storage at all. */
  readonly available: boolean;
  read(key: StorageKey): string | undefined;
  /** True when the value was stored. */
  write(key: StorageKey, value: string): boolean;
  remove(key: StorageKey): void;
}

/** Wraps a browser `Storage` (or its absence) so no call can throw. */
export function createStorage(backing: Storage | undefined): AppStorage {
  const attempt = <T>(fn: () => T, otherwise: T): T => {
    if (!backing) return otherwise;
    try {
      return fn();
    } catch {
      return otherwise;
    }
  };
  return {
    available: backing !== undefined,
    read: (key) => attempt(() => backing?.getItem(key) ?? undefined, undefined),
    write: (key, value) => attempt(() => { backing?.setItem(key, value); return true; }, false),
    remove: (key) => attempt(() => backing?.removeItem(key), undefined),
  };
}

/** The page's localStorage, or undefined where reading it throws (blocked site data). */
export function browserStorage(): Storage | undefined {
  try {
    return globalThis.localStorage ?? undefined;
  } catch {
    return undefined;
  }
}
```

`model/shop.ts`:

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// The user's shop (Domain model, ShopProfile: "per user, not per project"), kept in the browser
// (ADR 015, D4) and checked by the engine's own rule before use.

import { type ShopProfile, shopProblem } from "@kerf/core";
import { STARTER_SHOP } from "./starter";
import type { AppStorage } from "./storage";

/** The saved shop, or the starter shop with the reason the saved one could not be used. */
export function loadShop(storage: AppStorage): { shop: ShopProfile; problem?: string } {
  const text = storage.read("kerf.shop");
  if (text === undefined) return { shop: STARTER_SHOP };
  let parsed: unknown;
  try {
    parsed = JSON.parse(text);
  } catch {
    return { shop: STARTER_SHOP, problem: "Your saved shop could not be used (it was damaged), so the starter shop is in use." };
  }
  const problem = shopProblem(parsed);
  if (problem) return { shop: STARTER_SHOP, problem: `Your saved shop could not be used (${problem.message}), so the starter shop is in use.` };
  return { shop: parsed as ShopProfile };
}

export function saveShop(storage: AppStorage, shop: ShopProfile): void {
  storage.write("kerf.shop", JSON.stringify(shop));
}
```

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors

// Design spec, "Number fields"; ADR 015, rule 3. Values: 1in = 203200 units.

import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { exactLiteral, readCount, readLength, readPosition } from "./size";
import { startProject } from "./commands";

const bookcase = startProject("Bookcase", "imperial").project;

describe("readLength()", () => {
  it("reads 30\" as 30 inches and keeps the text as typed", () => {
    expect(readLength('30"', bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: true, expr: '30"', value: 6_096_000 });
  });

  it("reads an expression over the project's names: depth - 1\" is 11 inches", () => {
    // depth = 12in in the Bookcase template; 12 - 1 = 11" = 2235200.
    expect(readLength('depth - 1"', bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: true, expr: 'depth - 1"', value: 2_235_200 });
  });

  it("snaps to the 1/64\" grid, half to even", () => {
    // 1/128" is half a grid step: rounds to the even step, 0... so 1" + 1/128" → 1".
    expect(readLength('1in + 1in/128', bookcase)).toMatchObject({ ok: true, value: 203_200 });
  });

  it("refuses a bare number and says to add a unit", () => {
    expect(readLength("30", bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: false, message: '"30" is not a length; add a unit (in, " or mm)' });
  });

  it("refuses a size worked out from itself", () => {
    expect(readLength('width + 1"', bookcase, "width")).toEqual({ ok: false, message: "Width can't be worked out from itself." });
  });

  it("refuses text that is not a size, with the engine's message", () => {
    const r = readLength("abc", bookcase);
    expect(r.ok).toBe(false);
  });

  it("asks for a size when the field is empty", () => {
    expect(readLength("  ", bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: false, message: 'Type a length, like 30" or 762mm.' });
  });
});

describe("readCount()", () => {
  it("reads 5 as five", () => {
    expect(readCount("5", bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: true, expr: "5", value: 5 });
  });

  it("refuses a length where a count belongs", () => {
    expect(readCount('5"', bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: false, message: "Type a whole number, like 4." });
  });

  it("refuses a fraction of a shelf", () => {
    expect(readCount("5/2", bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: false, message: "Type a whole number, like 4." });
  });
});

describe("readPosition()", () => {
  it("accepts a fraction of the opening, kept as typed", () => {
    expect(readPosition("1/3", bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: true, expr: "1/3" });
  });

  it("accepts a length from the left", () => {
    expect(readPosition('12"', bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: true, expr: '12"' });
  });

  it("refuses a fraction of 1 or more", () => {
    expect(readPosition("4/3", bookcase)).toEqual({ ok: false, message: 'Type a length like 12" or a fraction like 1/3.' });
  });
});

describe("exactLiteral()", () => {
  it("writes 1/8\" as exactly 3.175mm", () => {
    // 25400 units ÷ 8000 per mm = 3.175 exactly.
    expect(exactLiteral(25_400)).toBe("3.175mm");
  });

  it("writes one 1/64\" step as exactly 0.396875mm", () => {
    expect(exactLiteral(3_175)).toBe("0.396875mm");
  });

  it("writes a whole number of millimetres without a decimal point", () => {
    expect(exactLiteral(80_000)).toBe("10mm");
  });
});
```

```ts
// The edits the app makes. Expected numbers: Operation specs, "Golden test: bookcase" and its

import { type Project, solve } from "@kerf/core";

const structureOf = (p: Project) => {

  if (!s) throw new Error("no structure");
  return s;
};
const shelfLine = (p: Project) => solve(p, shop).cutList.lines.find((l) => l.parts.some((x) => x.name === "Shelf"));

describe("startProject()", () => {

    const p = start().project;

    expect(setSize(p, structureOf(p).id, "w", 'width + 1"')).toEqual({ ok: false, message: "Width can't be worked out from itself." });

describe("setOption()", () => {

describe("acceptJoinery()", () => {

describe("addRoundover()", () => {

    const top = solve(p, shop).placements.find((x) => x.name === "Top");
    if (!top) throw new Error("no top");
    const { project, label } = addRoundover(p, top, ["edge1/face0", "edge1/face1"], IN / 8);
    expect(label).toBe('Roundover 1/8" on Top');
    const def = project.parts.find((d) => d.name === "Top");
    expect(def?.detached).toBe(true);
    expect(def?.ops).toMatchObject([{ type: "roundover", edges: ["edge1/face0", "edge1/face1"], radius: "3.175mm" }]);
  });
});

describe("setUnits()", () => {
  it("switches the project to millimetres, labelled", () => {
    const { project, label } = setUnits(start().project, "metric");
    expect(project.units).toBe("metric");
    expect(label).toBe("Units: millimetres");
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `model/size.ts`**

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// What a user types into a size or count field (design spec, "Number fields"; ADR 015, rule 3).
// The text is kept as typed (an expression), after the engine has checked it means the right kind
// of thing. Every message is the engine's or a plain instruction; nothing is silently dropped.

import { type Expr, ExpressionError, evaluateQuantity, type Len, type Project, type Quantity, snapToGrid, solveModel } from "@kerf/core";

export type Reading = { ok: true; expr: Expr; value: Len } | { ok: false; message: string };
export type CountReading = { ok: true; expr: Expr; value: number } | { ok: false; message: string };

const capitalised = (s: string): string => s.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + s.slice(1);

/** Evaluates `text` over the project's names, without `self` so a field cannot refer to itself. */
function evaluate(text: string, project: Project, self?: string): { q: Quantity } | { message: string } {
  const scope: Record<string, Quantity> = { ...solveModel(project).scope };
  if (self !== undefined) delete scope[self];
  try {
    return { q: evaluateQuantity(text, scope) };
  } catch (error) {
    if (!(error instanceof ExpressionError)) throw error;
    if (self !== undefined && new RegExp(`\\b${self}\\b`).test(text)) {
      return { message: `${capitalised(self)} can't be worked out from itself.` };
    }
    return { message: error.message };
  }
}

/** A length typed by the user, as the expression to store and its snapped value. */
export function readLength(text: string, project: Project, self?: string): Reading {
  if (text.trim() === "") return { ok: false, message: 'Type a length, like 30" or 762mm.' };
  const r = evaluate(text, project, self);
  if ("message" in r) return { ok: false, message: r.message };
  if (r.q.kind !== "length") return { ok: false, message: `"${text.trim()}" is not a length; add a unit (in, " or mm)` };
  return { ok: true, expr: text.trim(), value: snapToGrid(r.q.value, project.units) };
}

/** A whole number typed by the user (a count of shelves or bays). */
export function readCount(text: string, project: Project, self?: string): CountReading {
  const r = evaluate(text, project, self);
  if ("message" in r) return { ok: false, message: r.message };
  const { kind, value } = r.q;
  if (kind === "length" || value.den !== 1n || value.num < 0n) return { ok: false, message: "Type a whole number, like 4." };
  return { ok: true, expr: text.trim(), value: Number(value.num) };
}

/** A partition's place (ADR 014, D9): a length from the opening's left face, or a fraction of it. */
export function readPosition(text: string, project: Project): { ok: true; expr: Expr } | { ok: false; message: string } {
  const ask = 'Type a length like 12" or a fraction like 1/3.';
  if (text.trim() === "") return { ok: false, message: ask };
  const r = evaluate(text, project);
  if ("message" in r) return { ok: false, message: r.message };
  if (r.q.kind === "length") return { ok: true, expr: text.trim() };
  const { num, den } = r.q.value;
  return num > 0n && num < den ? { ok: true, expr: text.trim() } : { ok: false, message: ask };
}

/** The value of an expression in this project, snapped; undefined when it cannot be worked out. */
export function valueOf(expr: Expr, project: Project): Len | undefined {
  const r = readLength(expr, project);
  return r.ok ? r.value : undefined;
}

/**
 * A length in internal units as an exact millimetre literal. Every whole number of units is an
 * exact decimal of millimetres (8000 units = 1 mm = 2^6 × 5^3), so nothing is rounded.
 */
export function exactLiteral(len: Len): Expr {
  const sign = len < 0 ? "-" : "";
  const units = Math.abs(len);
  const whole = Math.trunc(units / 8000);
  const millionths = (units % 8000) * 125;
  if (millionths === 0) return `${sign}${whole}mm`;
  const fraction = millionths.toString().padStart(6, "0").replace(/0+$/, "");
  return `${sign}${whole}.${fraction}mm`;
}
```

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// The words the screens use for the forms' fields and choices (Interaction spec, "Shape" table and
// "Shop language"). The engine's option keys never reach the screen.

import type { Structure } from "@kerf/core";

export type SizeKey = "w" | "h" | "d";

/** A case is Width × Height × Depth; a box is Length × Width × Height (Interaction spec, "Start"). */
export function sizeLabel(type: Structure["type"], key: SizeKey): string {
  if (type === "case") return { w: "Width", h: "Height", d: "Depth" }[key];
  return { w: "Length", h: "Height", d: "Width" }[key];
}

/** The order the size fields are shown in. */
export function sizeOrder(type: Structure["type"]): SizeKey[] {
  return type === "case" ? ["w", "h", "d"] : ["w", "d", "h"];
}

export const CHOICES = {
  topBottom: [
    { value: "between-sides", label: "Between the sides" },
    { value: "sides-between", label: "Sides between the top and bottom" },
  ],
  back: [
    { value: "none", label: "None" },
    { value: "inset-rabbet", label: "Inset in rabbets" },
    { value: "applied", label: "Applied to the back edges" },
  ],
  cornerJoint: [
    { value: "through-dovetail", label: "Through dovetails" },
    { value: "rabbet", label: "Rabbeted corners" },
  ],
  bottom: [
    { value: "none", label: "None" },
    { value: "groove", label: "In a groove" },
  ],
} as const satisfies Record<string, ReadonlyArray<{ value: string; label: string }>>;

const OPTION_LABELS: Record<string, string> = {
  carcaseStock: "Carcase stock",
  backStock: "Back stock",
  wallStock: "Wall stock",
  bottomStock: "Bottom stock",
  topBottom: "Top and bottom",
  back: "Back",
  cornerJoint: "Corners",
  bottom: "Bottom",
  grainWraps: "Grain wraps the corners",
  dadoDepth: "Dado depth",
  rabbetDepth: "Rabbet depth",
  backRabbetDepth: "Back rabbet depth",
  grooveDepth: "Groove depth",
  grooveOffset: "Groove height",
  shelfRoundover: "Shelf roundover",
};

/** The shop name of a form field. */
export function optionLabel(key: string): string {
  return OPTION_LABELS[key] ?? key;
}

/** The shop words for a choice's value ("inset-rabbet" → "inset in rabbets"), lower case. */
export function choiceLabel(key: string, value: string): string {
  const list: ReadonlyArray<{ value: string; label: string }> | undefined = (CHOICES as Record<string, ReadonlyArray<{ value: string; label: string }>>)[key];
  return (list?.find((c) => c.value === value)?.label ?? value).toLowerCase();
}
```

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// Every change the user can make, as a pure function from a project to a new project and the
// History label for it (design spec, "History"; Interaction spec: "Undo covers every action").
// Nothing here mutates its input; the store records what these return.

import {
  detachCopyIn, detachPartIn, type EdgeRef, type Expr, formatLength, type Id, type Len, type Opening,
  type Param, type PlacedPart, type Project, projectFromTemplate, type Structure, TEMPLATES,
} from "@kerf/core";
import { newId } from "./ids";
import { choiceLabel, optionLabel, type SizeKey, sizeLabel } from "./labels";
import { exactLiteral, readCount, readLength, readPosition, valueOf } from "./size";
import { STARTER_STOCK, STOCK_FOR } from "./starter";

export interface Edit {
  project: Project;
  /** What History shows for this change, in shop words. */
  label: string;
}
export type EditResult = { ok: true; edit: Edit } | { ok: false; message: string };

export type TemplateName = "Bookcase" | "Keepsake box" | "Blank case" | "Blank box";

const IDENTIFIER = /^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$/;

/** A new project from a template, with the starter stock copied in. */
export function startProject(name: TemplateName, units: Project["units"]): Edit {
  const template = TEMPLATES.find((t) => t.name === name);
  if (!template) throw new RangeError(`There is no template named "${name}".`);
  const project = projectFromTemplate(template, { stock: structuredClone([...STARTER_STOCK]), stockFor: STOCK_FOR });
  return { project: { ...project, units }, label: `New ${name.toLowerCase()}` };
}

/** The project with one structure replaced by `change(structure)`. */
function withStructure(project: Project, id: Id, change: (s: Structure) => Structure): Project {
  return { ...project, structures: project.structures.map((s) => (s.id === id ? change(structuredClone(s)) : s)) };
}

function structureIn(project: Project, id: Id): Structure {
  const found = project.structures.find((s) => s.id === id);
  if (!found) throw new RangeError(`The project has no structure "${id}".`);
  return found;
}

/** The parameter a form field names (`width`), if it names one. */
function paramNamed(project: Project, expr: Expr): Param | undefined {
  return IDENTIFIER.test(expr) ? project.params.find((p) => p.name === expr) : undefined;
}

function setParam(project: Project, param: Param, expr: Expr): Project {
  return { ...project, params: project.params.map((p) => (p.id === param.id ? { ...p, expr } : p)) };
}

/** Changes one overall size, typed by the user (ADR 015, rule 3: a named parameter is edited). */
export function setSize(project: Project, structure: Id, key: SizeKey, text: string): EditResult {
  const s = structureIn(project, structure);
  const current = s.size[key];
  const label = sizeLabel(s.type, key);
  const param = paramNamed(project, current);
  const read = readLength(text, project, param?.name);
  if (!read.ok) return { ok: false, message: read.message.replace(/^\w+ can't/, `${label} can't`) };
  const before = valueOf(current, project);
  const next = param
    ? setParam(project, param, read.expr)
    : withStructure(project, structure, (st) => ({ ...st, size: { ...st.size, [key]: read.expr } }));
  const shown = (v: Len | undefined): string => (v === undefined ? "?" : formatLength(v, project.units));
  return { ok: true, edit: { project: next, label: `${label} ${shown(before)} → ${shown(read.value)}` } };
}

/** Sets a form option (a choice, a stock, a flag, a depth). */
export function setOption(project: Project, structure: Id, key: string, value: string | boolean): Edit {
  const next = withStructure(project, structure, (s) => ({ ...s, options: { ...s.options, [key]: value } }));
  const stock = typeof value === "string" ? project.stock.find((x) => x.id === value) : undefined;
  const shown = typeof value === "boolean" ? (value ? "on" : "off") : (stock?.name ?? choiceLabel(key, value));
  return { project: next, label: `${optionLabel(key)}: ${shown}` };
}

/** The project with one opening (anywhere in the tree) replaced. */
function withOpening(project: Project, structure: Id, opening: Id, change: (o: Opening) => Opening): Project {
  const walk = (list: Opening[]): Opening[] => list.map((o) => (o.id === opening ? change(o) : { ...o, children: walk(o.children) }));
  return withStructure(project, structure, (s) => ({ ...s, openings: walk(s.openings) }));
}

function openingIn(project: Project, structure: Id, opening: Id): Opening | undefined {
  const find = (list: Opening[]): Opening | undefined => {
    for (const o of list) {
      if (o.id === opening) return o;
      const inner = find(o.children);
      if (inner) return inner;
    }
    return undefined;
  };
  return find(structureIn(project, structure).openings);
}

/** Shelves in an opening: a new division, or a new count for the shelves already there. */
export function setShelves(project: Project, structure: Id, opening: Id, countText: string, spacing: "even" | Expr[]): EditResult {
  const o = openingIn(project, structure, opening);
  const existing = o?.division?.type === "shelves" ? o.division : undefined;
  const param = existing && paramNamed(project, existing.count);
  const read = readCount(countText, project, param?.name);
  if (!read.ok) return { ok: false, message: read.message };
  const before = existing ? readCount(existing.count, project) : undefined;
  const counted = param ? setParam(project, param, read.expr) : project;
  const count = param ? param.name : read.expr;
  const next = withOpening(counted, structure, opening, (x) => ({
    ...x,
    division: { type: "shelves", count, spacing },
    children: existing ? x.children : [],
  }));
  const label = before?.ok ? `Shelves ${before.value} → ${read.value}` : `Added ${read.value} shelves`;
  return { ok: true, edit: { project: next, label } };
}

/** A vertical partition at a length or a fraction ("1/3") from the opening's left face (ADR 014, D9). */
export function addPartition(project: Project, structure: Id, opening: Id, atText: string): EditResult {
  const read = readPosition(atText, project);
  if (!read.ok) return { ok: false, message: read.message };
  const children: Opening[] = [{ id: newId(), children: [] }, { id: newId(), children: [] }];
  const next = withOpening(project, structure, opening, (x) => ({ ...x, division: { type: "partition", at: read.expr }, children }));
  return { ok: true, edit: { project: next, label: `Added a partition at ${read.expr}` } };
}

/** N equal bays across the opening. */
export function splitBays(project: Project, structure: Id, opening: Id, countText: string): EditResult {
  const read = readCount(countText, project);
  if (!read.ok) return { ok: false, message: read.message };
  if (read.value < 2) return { ok: false, message: "Bays need a number of 2 or more." };
  const children: Opening[] = Array.from({ length: read.value }, () => ({ id: newId(), children: [] }));
  const next = withOpening(project, structure, opening, (x) => ({ ...x, division: { type: "bays", count: read.expr }, children }));
  return { ok: true, edit: { project: next, label: `Split into ${read.value} bays` } };
}

/** Empties an opening again. */
export function clearDivision(project: Project, structure: Id, opening: Id): Edit {
  const next = withOpening(project, structure, opening, ({ division: _, ...rest }) => ({ ...rest, children: [] }));
  return { project: next, label: "Emptied the opening" };
}

/** "Accept all" (ADR 015, D2): every form's joinery is marked reviewed; no board changes. */
export function acceptJoinery(project: Project): Edit {
  return {
    project: { ...project, structures: project.structures.map((s) => ({ ...s, options: { ...s.options, joineryReviewed: true } })) },
    label: "Accepted the joinery",
  };
}

/** The form's shelf roundover (ADR 015, D3); null removes it. */
export function setShelfRoundover(project: Project, structure: Id, radius: Len | null): Edit {
  const next = withStructure(project, structure, (s) => {
    const { shelfRoundover: _, ...rest } = s.options;
    return { ...s, options: radius === null ? rest : { ...rest, shelfRoundover: exactLiteral(radius) } };
  });
  return { project: next, label: radius === null ? "No shelf roundover" : `Shelf roundover ${formatLength(radius, project.units)}` };
}

/**
 * Takes over a board the form made so it can be edited by hand (Interaction spec, "Tweak":
 * "detaches only that board"). A member of a row is taken out of the row on its own.
 */
export function detachForEdit(project: Project, placed: PlacedPart): { project: Project; detached: boolean } {
  if (!placed.source || placed.detached) return { project, detached: false };
  if (placed.pattern) return { project: detachCopyIn(project, placed.pattern.id, placed.pattern.index), detached: true };
  return { project: detachPartIn(project, placed.part), detached: true };
}

/** A roundover on a board made by hand or taken over (ADR 015, D3). */
export function addRoundover(project: Project, placed: PlacedPart, edges: EdgeRef[], radius: Len): Edit {
  const taken = detachForEdit(project, placed);
  const partId = taken.project.instances.find((i) => i.id === placed.instance)?.def ?? placed.part;
  const op = { id: newId(), type: "roundover" as const, edges, radius: exactLiteral(radius) };
  const next = { ...taken.project, parts: taken.project.parts.map((d) => (d.id === partId ? { ...d, ops: [...d.ops, op] } : d)) };
  return { project: next, label: `Roundover ${formatLength(radius, project.units)} on ${placed.name}` };
}

/** Inches or millimetres for this project (ADR 015, rule 4). */
export function setUnits(project: Project, units: Project["units"]): Edit {
  return { project: { ...project, units }, label: `Units: ${units === "imperial" ? "inches" : "millimetres"}` };
}
```

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// Interaction spec, "History panel": "a readable list of every change … click any entry to step
// back, like Photoshop's History". ADR 015, rule 1 (session only, last 200 changes).

import type { Project } from "@kerf/core";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { canRedo, canUndo, currentProject, HISTORY_LIMIT, jumpTo, record, redo, startHistory, undo } from "./history";

const p = (name: string): Project => ({ schemaVersion: 1, name, units: "imperial", params: [], stock: [], parts: [], instances: [], patterns: [], joints: [], matchSets: [], structures: [] });
const edit = (label: string) => ({ label, project: p(label) });

describe("history", () => {
  it("starts with one entry and nothing to undo", () => {
    const h = startHistory(edit("New bookcase"));
    expect(h.entries.map((e) => e.label)).toEqual(["New bookcase"]);
    expect(canUndo(h)).toBe(false);
  });

  it("undoes and redoes one change", () => {
    const h = record(startHistory(edit("New bookcase")), edit('Width 36" → 30"'));
    expect(currentProject(undo(h)).name).toBe("New bookcase");
    expect(currentProject(redo(undo(h))).name).toBe('Width 36" → 30"');
  });

  it("drops the later entries when a change is made after stepping back", () => {
    let h = startHistory(edit("a"));
    h = record(record(h, edit("b")), edit("c"));
    h = record(jumpTo(h, 0), edit("d"));
    expect(h.entries.map((e) => e.label)).toEqual(["a", "d"]);
    expect(canRedo(h)).toBe(false);
  });

  it("steps back to any entry", () => {
    let h = startHistory(edit("a"));
    h = record(record(h, edit("b")), edit("c"));
    expect(currentProject(jumpTo(h, 1)).name).toBe("b");
  });

  it("ignores undo at the start and redo at the end", () => {
    const h = startHistory(edit("a"));
    expect(undo(h)).toBe(h);
    expect(redo(h)).toBe(h);
  });

  it("refuses to jump to an entry that does not exist", () => {
    expect(() => jumpTo(startHistory(edit("a")), 5)).toThrow(RangeError);
  });

  it("keeps the last 200 changes, dropping the oldest", () => {
    let h = startHistory(edit("0"));
    for (let i = 1; i <= HISTORY_LIMIT + 5; i += 1) h = record(h, edit(String(i)));
    expect(h.entries).toHaveLength(HISTORY_LIMIT);
    expect(h.entries[0]?.label).toBe("6");
    expect(currentProject(h).name).toBe(String(HISTORY_LIMIT + 5));
  });
});
```

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
// Copyright (C) 2026 Kerf contributors
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only

// The History list (Interaction spec, "History panel"; ADR 015, rule 1): one whole project per
// named change. Projects are small and immutable here, so keeping each one makes stepping back
// exact and simple. A value, never mutated: every function returns a new History.

import type { Project } from "@kerf/core";
import type { Edit } from "./commands";

export interface HistoryEntry {
  label: string;
  project: Project;
}

export interface History {
  readonly entries: readonly HistoryEntry[];
  /** The entry being shown. */
  readonly at: number;
}

/** How many changes are kept (ADR 015, rule 1); the oldest is dropped beyond this. */
export const HISTORY_LIMIT = 200;

export function startHistory(edit: Edit): History {
  return { entries: [{ label: edit.label, project: edit.project }], at: 0 };
}

/** Adds a change after the current entry, dropping any entries after it. */
export function record(history: History, edit: Edit): History {
  const kept = [...history.entries.slice(0, history.at + 1), { label: edit.label, project: edit.project }];
  const entries = kept.slice(Math.max(0, kept.length - HISTORY_LIMIT));
  return { entries, at: entries.length - 1 };
}

export const canUndo = (h: History): boolean => h.at > 0;
export const canRedo = (h: History): boolean => h.at < h.entries.length - 1;

export function undo(h: History): History {
  return canUndo(h) ? { ...h, at: h.at - 1 } : h;
}

export function redo(h: History): History {
  return canRedo(h) ? { ...h, at: h.at + 1 } : h;
}

/** Shows entry `index`. Throws RangeError for an index outside the list. */
export function jumpTo(h: History, index: number): History {
  if (!Number.isInteger(index) || index < 0 || index >= h.entries.length) {
    throw new RangeError(`History has no entry ${index}.`);
  }
  return { ...h, at: index };
}

export function currentProject(h: History): Project {
  const entry = h.entries[h.at];
  if (!entry) throw new RangeError("History is empty.");
  return entry.project;
}
```

```ts
// Kerf — free, open-source woodworking design tool
```
